# Third-party notices

The repository's Apache-2.0 license applies to original project work. The
third-party material below keeps its own license and copyright notices.

Components have been adapted for this project. |

separate scanner/rule licenses, source provenance, and update process. Their
licenses do not replace the application's license.

## Fonts, icons, emoji, and external content

- **Geist:** Copyright 2024 The Geist Project Authors. The font files delivered
  by `@fontsource-variable/geist` remain under SIL OFL-1.1. The full font license
  and copyright notice are included in the generated dependency section below.
- **Lucide and Feather:** Lucide's ISC notice and the MIT notice for its
  Feather-derived icons are preserved together in the dependency section below.
  The hand-written SVG wrappers in `src/components/Icons.tsx` also include
  Feather-style icon paths; preserve these notices when redistributing them.
- **Twemoji:** Graphics are by Twitter and other contributors, maintained by
  Jason Sofonia, Justine De Caires, and the Twemoji community. The app loads
  unmodified [Twemoji 17.0.3 SVG artwork](https://github.com/jdecked/twemoji/tree/v17.0.3/assets/svg)
  through jsDelivr. The emoji picker uses the Twitter artwork supplied through
  [emoji-datasource](https://github.com/iamcal/emoji-data). Graphics remain under
  [CC-BY-4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/); see the
  [upstream graphics license](https://github.com/jdecked/twemoji/blob/v17.0.3/LICENSE-GRAPHICS).
  The Twemoji JavaScript code is MIT licensed; its copyright and permission
  notice are included below. This attribution does not imply endorsement.
- **Avatar SVGs:** `public/avatars/` contains six local SVG files without
  third-party attribution metadata. Their author/source should be confirmed
  before release; the app license grants only rights held by the contributors.
- **Provider content:** Music, album artwork, GIFs, user uploads, and remotely
  loaded provider players/SDKs retain their owners' rights and provider terms.
  They are not relicensed by the repository's Apache-2.0 license. Provider SDKs
  are loaded from their providers rather than copied into this repository.

## Scope of dependency notices

The section below inventories production and development dependencies from the
npm lockfile and copies license, copyright, and notice documents from matching
installed package versions. Build tools are included because their helpers or
CSS can be emitted into browser assets. This is not a list of code actually
included by the browser bundler.

After `npm ci`, regenerate it with:

```sh
node scripts/generate-third-party-notices.mjs
node scripts/generate-third-party-notices.mjs --check
```

The generator reads local files only and adds no dependencies. Optional packages
for other operating systems remain in the inventory, marked as not installed.
Regenerate on the target platform when distributing a built artifact. To copy
the notices and the root `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` with a web build, run the
following after building:

```sh
node scripts/generate-third-party-notices.mjs --bundle dist
```

The bundle command assembles current target-platform notices without requiring
the committed snapshot to match that platform. `--check` checks the current
installation's output, so a different platform can produce a different snapshot.
Downloaded npm packages retain their supplied licenses; they are not vendored
into the source repository.

Sentry's JavaScript SDKs and build plugin use MIT. The build-time `@sentry/cli`
and its platform binaries use [FSL-1.1-MIT](https://github.com/getsentry/sentry-cli/blob/master/LICENSE).
That license expressly permits internal use, excludes its defined competing
commercial uses, and requires retaining its terms and copyright on redistribution.
Each CLI version receives an additional MIT license two years after its release.
The supplied terms are retained in the generated inventory below.

The npm inventory does not enumerate libraries embedded inside native/Wasm
binaries, operating-system packages in container images, separately downloaded
agent engines, Python scanner dependencies, or content fetched at runtime.
In particular, sharp's prebuilt binaries include libvips and other libraries
with LGPL and additional licenses. Their package README licensing tables are
preserved below, but those tables are not complete license/source bundles.
Before distributing binaries or container images, collect the exact target
platform's notices and satisfy the applicable source/relinking requirements.
Lightning CSS is an MPL-2.0 build tool; distributing that tool also requires
preserving its separate terms and source availability.

Keep this file, the root license, and the bundled notices with source releases.
Include the relevant license texts and asset attribution with compiled releases.

The optional Linux build helper `@napi-rs/lzma-linux-x64-gnu@1.5.1` and its
same-version parent declare MIT but supply no license or copyright notice in
their distributions or matching source tree. The
[reviewed notice](licenses/npm/lzma-1.5.1/NOTICE.txt) preserves that declaration
and separately identified standard MIT terms; its
[provenance](licenses/npm/lzma-1.5.1/provenance.json) records exact integrity and
source metadata. No copyright holder or year was invented. Upstream notice
clarification and native-binary review remain required before public binary
distribution. The notice generator accepts only the reviewed version, license,
integrity, and notice hash; other missing notices still fail.
